- **Hum** (`ground/hum.ts`, `audio/synth.ts` hum families, `LoopEmitter.rate`): uncommon+ drops are positional loops in the existing loop pool: weapons/armor/ingots/arrows ring metallic, gems chime (even common ones, softly), tomes/blueprints/quest items whisper, the rest a warm drone, Bitchin' a shimmering chord; one note per rarity (rising pentatonic), level and reach by rarity (uncommon ~9 m, legendary ~34 m, Bitchin' ~56 m), muffled indoors and in caves (the engine resolves the emitter's space), within the 8-voice cap so the nearest/rarest win. The existing grant sting plays on taking.
- **Ownership** (unchanged, documented): rolled loot (kills, chests) is personal: only its owner sees it and can take it, so nothing can be stolen and no reservation timer is needed; dropped items (G / drag out) are public, everyone near sees the same item, rarity, model and beam in the same place, first E wins (the loser gets 403/2 "That item is gone"). Rare+ pickups ride the snapshot **mid ring** (200 m) so beams are seen from afar (static: almost free after first sight); the scale bots never loot, so `LODESTONE_WIRE_DIGEST=1` is unchanged. Despawn 10 minutes as before.

**Evidence** (`tools/playtest/ground-loot-shots.mjs`, private stack with `LODESTONE_ZONE_DEV=1`, two players in one headless Chromium): 7/7 checks in `docs/evidence/pt2-ground-loot.json`: a wolf camp killed, its loot scattered (a relic sword hovering in its beam, spears in the grass) and invisible to the other player; A takes a spear with E; a chest pops its items out; a line of every rarity dropped by A is seen by both with identical id, item, rarity and position; from 127 m at night B still sees the six rare+ beams and not the commoner drops; A takes the legendary and it is gone for B. Shots `docs/evidence/pt2-ground-loot-{kill-day,chest-pop,chest-day,shared-day-A,shared-day-B,closeup-day,shared-night-A,shared-night-B,far-night-B,forty-day}.jpg`. **Perf**: the same view before/after dropping 40 mixed-rarity items (High, 1280x720, vsync): draw calls 344 -> 378 (40 models + 4 light batches, no per-drop shadow pass), p50 16.7 ms both, frames over 33 ms 13 before / 1 after (the GPU is shared with other agents' browsers: noise dominates). Turn bench (`client/src/world/bench/turn.mjs high`, uncapped, solo): 0 long tasks; pan p50 16.1 / p99 41.8 ms on a heavily shared machine (quiet-machine runs were ~4.5 ms), so only the long-task count is comparable.

**Tests**: Go `server/zone/internal/game/loot_ground_test.go` (walk-over rules table, scatter spread, kill loot scattered and personal, the setting and its echo, settle time, chest items on the ground and taken with E, the drop race, quiet full bag, despawn, rare drops across the mid ring with both clients agreeing); WP14 tests adjusted (boss loot taken with E, chest item levels read from the ground). Vitest `client/src/ui/inventory/ground/ground.test.ts` (20: beam params by rarity and tier, a model and real geometry for every item key, common and Bitchin', armor pieces centred, hover glow, coin piles, hum families/notes/levels/ranges, cave muffling, loop-pool priority, the batched lights' four draw calls and rebuild rules, auto-collect sync).

**Gates** (main merged in at `8d7ffc1`): `gen:check` ok, `test:golden` PASS (wp01 evidence restored), `go vet ./zone/...` clean, `go test ./zone/internal/... ./pkg/...` green, `go test ./zone/` green except `TestZoneProcessTerrain` and (when run beside other process tests) `TestG4LoopForgeSunsteelBlade`, both green rerun alone (load timing; the WP14 kill-mid-grant and drop/pickup/despawn process tests pass with chest items on the ground: Postgres holds the starter kit plus each chest's gold exactly once), client typecheck ok, `eslint .` clean, client vitest 100 files / 1239 tests, `node scripts/whatsnew.mjs check` (31 entries, `pt2-ground-loot` first).

Files: new `server/zone/internal/game/loot_ground{,_test}.go`, `client/src/ui/inventory/ground/{beam,models,hum,autoLoot,ground.test}.ts`, `tools/playtest/ground-loot-shots.mjs`, `docs/cr/037-ground-loot.md`, `docs/evidence/pt2-ground-loot*`; changed `server/zone/internal/game/{loot,items,mining,snapshots}.go` (+ tests `items_test.go`, `loot_level_test.go`), `client/src/ui/inventory/{LootLayer.tsx,events.ts,link.ts,README.md}`, `client/src/combat/armor/model.ts`, `client/src/audio/{synth,spatial,loopPool,loops,engine}.ts` + README; shared (small): `client/src/world/warmExtras.ts`, `client/src/ui/menu/EscMenu.tsx` (the toggle row), `contracts/events.md`, `server/zone/README.md`, `client/src/ui/whatsnew/changelog.json`.

Gaps: rolled loot left on the ground is lost on a zone crash/restart (as kill loot always was; dropped items persist); personal drops are not mirrored across WP16 ghost bands (as before); the pop-out arc is a client heuristic (a pickup newer than anything seen, within 45 m), so a drop first seen when walking into range does not arc; the hum loops share the 8 voices with fires and water.

## Playtest 2: co-op playthrough ✅ (branch `pt2/coop-playtest`)

Nicole: "go playtest mode, focused on the two player experience, trying to beat the game". Two fresh characters (Nell and Rook) played the prologue and all six acts of "The Last Song" in a party on a private stack, through the real client, from the Haven to the Hush's ending and credits. Play log, per-act fun/clarity scores before and after, findings and every dev-console use: **`docs/evidence/pt2-coop-playtest.md`** (raw timestamped log `pt2-coop-playtest-log.txt`, screenshots `pt2-coop-*.jpg`). Dev console only for travel (`/tp` where my walker stuck, said each time), one `/give` to retry the mining step after its fix, and `/god` for the last Hush run (my bots could not survive it unassisted; the mechanics, length and ending were then played).

Scores (fun/clarity, before → after): prologue 5/5 → 7/7, I 6/5 → 7/7, II 3/4 → 6/6, III 4/5 → 7/6, IV 5/4 → 7/6, V 3/4 → 5/5, VI 4/6 → 7/8 (assisted; the entrance and ending are the best moments of the game).

Fixes (each its own commit, with tests):
- **Co-op**: the prologue's talk steps are shared in a party (`e344c88`); party members within 30 m of a breaking vein get their own drop (`457d5ef`); story townsfolk make no side offers while the story needs them and "The Lodestone's Hum" (the story's own premise) is retired once it begins: its card sat over a fight eating every click (`8e54b7c`).
- **Bugs**: the sunlit shaft was a lid you walked across (cave ground: the old surface only for missing cells; Go and TS, one golden floor vector in `golden.json` and `golden-v1.json`) (`b49dad6`, `20cfd3c`); under the ground where no cave is, the floor is the surface (a rider fell 330 m through the world; the ride step that tunnelled is not found) (`d56c403`); the sanctum's lens on its guardian's fall, not "the place falls quiet" (completed with the Archon at 99 %) (`a2a22de`).
- **Pacing**: crystal towers stay dry to climb (rain made them unclimbable: 13 minutes on one step; Go `weather.Sheltered` in the zone's step env, TS `shelteredField` in the client's) (`6349315`); a tower top is a respawn point (`2c734c6`); Act II's forks must be walkable to, under 44 degrees (the Howling Crags' summit tower is replaced by The Hollow Spire on the official world) (`67342a8`); Ulus hands each player a spirit whistle at the end of Act I (`411765a`).
- **Balance**: draughts heal a share of the health pool (40 % for a Healing Draught; a flat 40 was a sixth of a level 12 bar) (`f157238`); the Hush's shroud breaks after 4 + 4 sunsteel/Light hits per player (8 % of its level- and party-scaled health was ~4,500 in 10 s for two level 30s) (`2b02de8`).
- **Clarity**: Act I's marker on the Last Keeper's stone (`ea3ed22`); the Starless Sovereign walks out within its aggro of a defender (it stood 80 m out in the dark for 8 minutes) (`0edddcd`); the level-up fanfare waits for a boss fight (it covered the endless night's warning) (`c441eb4`).
- **Tools** (`0d90a61` and later): `tools/playtest/coop-driver.mjs` keeps two players alive while commands arrive over a local port (`coop-send.mjs`), `coop-helpers.mjs` (grid A* around town walls from the new towns debug `solids`, talk, buy, fight with focus/revive/draughts/dodge, climb, mine, forge, travel with a logged `/tp` fallback, gear), `zone-restart.mjs` (rebuild and restart only a playtest stack's zone, keeping its database).

Open (in the log, not fixed): whether a human pair survives the Hush (play it by hand; the levers would be its level or rend/toll damage); the Galeborn Archon is the least fun fight for a melee pair; level gates never bound (level 30 at Act VI's gate 14); fast travel only to crystals you attuned yourself; respawns put friends at their own last waypoints (3 km apart); "1 in stock" in shops reads as exclusive though stock is not tracked; the pack doesn't flag better gear. For the immersion pass: world event cards sit over the clock for minutes; Gloamlings stand in the Hush's entrance; "Dodged!" toasts for unseen lightning mid-boss.

Suites after merging main (ground loot, voice settings, composer): `gen:check` up to date; `node scripts/whatsnew.mjs check` (32 entries, `pt2-coop` first); go vet clean (`server/pkg/{caves,weather,movement}`, `server/zone/...`); go test `server/pkg/{caves,weather,movement,worldhost}` ok, `server/zone/...` ok except five timing tests that failed under load (golden, vitest and Go running at once: `TestZoneProcessPublishesEarshot`, `TestHandoffAcrossBorderKeepsPredictionAndIdentity`, `TestEarshotHandoffCadence`, `TestHeldStreamReachesNearObserversOnly`, `TestEnemiesDoNotWalkThroughBuildings`) and passed rerun alone; client typecheck ok (`tools/creator` fails only in a worktree, ajv, as before); `eslint .` clean; client vitest 101 files / 1241 tests; `npm run test:golden`: every hash matches in every host (Node, Chromium, Go compiler and interpreter, native Rust, the archived genVersion 1 in Node and Go: 0 failures), but the Chromium step exits 1 on its chunk-time budget (p95 9.6-10.7 ms vs 8 ms, rerun alone twice) with other agents' Go, vitest and cargo jobs loading the machine; no chunk generation code changed here (the cave fix is collision only); wp01 evidence restored.

## Playtest 2: calm kills ✅ (branch `pt2/kill-flash`)

Nicole (accessibility bug): "when the enemies die, it causes a horrible flashing that is overstimulating for my friend. whenever i kill an enemy". Client only: no contract, protocol, server or migration change. Docs: `client/src/combat/README.md` "Calm kills: flash safety".

**What was flashing** (found with the new harness, frame by frame; none of it was a whole-frame pulse at a sword kill in daylight, which is why the earlier screencast saw nothing):
- **Every hit turned the whole enemy white** for 130 ms (`emissive += 1.0` white, through the bloom), restarted by every hit: a wand (4 hits/s), a stream (Cinder Spray 12/s, Arc Sparks 15/s) or a pack strobed the body. A baseline run counted 7-16 white-flash onsets per 16 s fight. The killing blow flashed it once more as the death started.
- **Each hit popped one to three HDR flash sprites on the same spot in the same frame**: the shot's impact, the weapon / spell hit burst and, for a second player, the Event 200 burst; each came on at full strength on its first frame, plus a point light that switched on at full intensity.
- **Your own shot at the lens**: a shot leaves your hand and eases onto the eye line, so for a frame or two its HDR core sat 0.3-1 m from the camera: a white diamond by the crosshair on every wand bolt (12-15 a second in a stream) and, for a Fireball, a cream disc over a third of the view (**a 28 % full-frame luminance jump** in one frame).
- Streams: the shock status shell (a yellow ellipse that filled half the view at a close enemy) switched on and off as the stream re-applied it; 16 lightning crackles a second on a shocked body; Arc Sparks' impact bolts on every hit.
- Pyre Brand's death bursts (CR 020) chained explosions through a pack: two flash sprites, a light and a kick per death.
- Fresh particles start white-hot (x1.8 brightness, 35 % white), so every burst began as a white blob through the bloom; HDR cores at 2.2x on high graphics.
- Melee: the world swing arc was a band of white-hot glow puffs sweeping across the first-person view 2-3 times a second.
- Smaller: the off-hand / wand-tip glow blinked with every shot; the enemy wind-up tell pulsed at 3.2 Hz; a summon running out flickered at 2.7 Hz; enemy telegraph decals popped on at 20 % and vanished at 65 % (a red patch at your feet); the death break-up of demons and golems drew a 2.5x HDR edge; death motes glowed at 2.2x.
- Checked and not flashing: the XP / level-up / quest / achievement HUD (slide-ins, no strobe), the camera (no auto-exposure), hitstop (a 60 ms hold, no frame cut), no material compile on a death (no frame gap over 100 ms at a kill in the killer's runs; the debris and every enemy program are in the warm-up). Loot beams belong to the ground-loot branch and were left alone (`LootLayer` untouched).

**Fixes** (`client/src/combat/flashSafety.ts`: one profile read by everything below):
- Enemy hits are a **soft tint of the body's own colour plus a warm rim** (peak 0.28 calm / 0.5 punchy, was 1.0 white) that eases in over 70 ms, **holds while hits keep coming** and fades over ~0.3 s; **a new pulse at most every 480 ms (340 ms punchy) per enemy** (`HitTint`): a stream is a steady glow, never a strobe. The killing blow does not re-flash a dying body.
- **One flash sprite and one light pulse per 2.5 m spot per 480 / 340 ms** (`FlashGate` in `fx/transients.ts`): the duplicates of one hit merge; a repeat impact on the same spot gets a small burst without flash, light, lightning bolts or kick. Flash sprites ease in (no one-frame pop), are dimmer (0.32 / 0.6 opacity) and, calm, under the bloom threshold (colours <= 0.9); lights ramp up and never steal a bright slot.
- Your own shot's **core and halo are gone within 0.7 m of the camera** and full from 2.5 m.
- Particles: fresh ones barely white-hot (`uHotK` 0.3 calm); spell HDR capped at 1.35 calm (2.2 on high before).
- Status shells ease in and out and fade at the camera; 2-3 shock crackles a second; swing arcs thin and steel-coloured; the hand glow holds through repeated shots; the tell pulses at 1.2-2 Hz; a summon fades at <= 1.3 Hz; telegraphs ease in and fade out; deaths have no hot edge (calm), dimmer motes, 40 % fewer pieces; shake halves with Reduce flashing (none with reduced motion, as before).
- **"Reduce flashing"** (Graphics screen, `stores/accessibility.ts`, saved on the device): **on by default**. Off allows the punchier profile, which keeps every rule above at <= 3 a second. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` forces the calm profile either way (the row's hint says so).

**Measurements** (`tools/playtest/flash-audit.mjs`: a private dev stack, high graphics, 13:00 clear unless noted, 1280x720, the Chrome DevTools screencast at 40-60 fps through 16 s of fighting 3-6 slimes / boars and 2 s of their deaths, analysed per pixel on a 64x36 raster. WCAG 2.3.1 general flashes = pairs of opposing >= 0.1 relative-luminance changes over >= 25 % of a 10-degree field (overlapping 1/3 x 1/3 windows), red flashes likewise; "compensated" follows the world through camera turns so the view sweeping over grass and sky is not counted; the worst 1 s window). Before = the branch base `8d7ffc1`, after = this branch, both with real cooldowns. Evidence: `docs/evidence/pt2-flash-audit-{before,after,after-punchy,after-control,before-observer,after-observer}.json`; contact sheets `pt2-flash-audit-{before,after}-{wand,arc-sparks,cinder-spray,fireball,pyre-brand,sword}.jpg` and `-{before,after}-observer-wand.jpg` (the frame with the biggest local change, its neighbours and the luminance trace).

| the killer's view | WCAG flashes/s compensated (raw): before -> after (punchy) | biggest one-frame jump of the full frame | brightest 1/144 cell jump |
|---|---|---|---|
| wand | 2 (5) -> **0 (1)** (0) | 0.034 -> 0.022 | 0.82 -> 0.53 |
| staff | 3 (3) -> **0 (3)** (0) | 0.036 -> 0.024 | 0.63 -> 0.56 |
| bow | 1 (1) -> 0 (2) (1) | 0.031 -> 0.024 | 0.64 -> 0.52 |
| wand, boar pack, 23:00 | 4 (6) -> **2 (3)** (2) | **0.221 -> 0.011** | 0.90 -> 0.52 |
| Cinder Spray (fire stream, 12/s) | **13 (12) -> 1 (3)** | 0.033 -> 0.025 | 0.85 -> 0.71 |
| Arc Sparks (lightning stream, 15/s) | **14 (14) -> 3 (4)** (3) | 0.098 -> 0.036 | 0.70 -> 0.72 |
| Starfall Hail (rain) | 0 (1) -> 0 (3) | 0.024 -> 0.030 | 0.34 -> 0.71 |
| Fireball | 1 (3) -> 1 (3) | **0.280 -> 0.085** | 0.98 -> 0.80 |
| Pyre Brand on a pack of 5 | 2 (3) -> **1 (1)** (1) | **0.101 -> 0.030** | 0.83 -> 0.60 |
| sword | 4 (6) -> 5 (8) (3) | 0.043 -> 0.034 | 0.79 -> 0.75 |
| spear | 3 (6) -> 4 (7) | 0.036 -> 0.035 | 0.81 -> 0.72 |
| sword swinging at nothing (control) | 3 (4) -> 2 (4) (3) | 0.024 -> 0.021 | 0.80 -> 0.40 |

A second player 4 m to the side (`--observer`, the same fights from their screen): 0-1 general flashes/s in every scenario before and after; their biggest full-frame jump wand 0.010 -> 0.004, Pyre Brand 0.060 -> 0.041, Arc Sparks 0.071 -> 0.014. Red flashes 0-1/s everywhere (compensated), before and after.

Reading it: every ranged weapon and spell now passes WCAG 2.3.1 in the killer's own view, the streams fell from 13-14 flashes a second to 1-3, and the one-frame full-view jumps (a Fireball leaving your hand, a pack dying at night, a brand chain) are gone. Melee still counts 4-5/s because the first-person sword and the walk through high-contrast grass move large areas of the picture: the control (the same swings at nothing) counts 2-3/s and the second player sees 1/s, so what is left is the weapon in your hand, not a flash (the white body, the white burst and the white swing band are gone from its frames). The brightest-cell column is noisy (the camera differs per run) and is a sanity check, not a target.

**Tests**: vitest `combat/flashSafety.test.ts` (profile choice incl. reduced motion; both profiles <= 3/s for pulses, gates, tell and fades; calm under the bloom threshold; a strobe of hits every 66-700 ms never exceeds 3 flashes/s or the peak; a stream holds the tint; a single hit eases in and out; the gate merges one hit's duplicates and passes <= 3/s into one spot; smooth flash / light envelopes), `enemies/enemies.test.ts` (the tint runs through hitstop, is capped, holds through a 15 Hz stream, a kill fades it); the fx suite passes unchanged.

Gaps: the WCAG count is an approximation (64x36 raster, integer-pixel camera compensation, no zoom compensation, a 10-degree field taken as 1/3 x 1/3 of a 1280x720 view); melee is not separable from the view model by the harness (see the control); a second player's first sight of a remote Arc Sparks stream hitched 1-2.5 s in both runs (headless, before and after: a first draw, not a flash; not investigated); loot beams are another branch's.

Shared files touched (small): `client/src/combat/{CombatLayer.tsx,README.md}`, `combat/enemies/{anim,material,renderer}.ts`, `combat/fx/{core,engine,particles,recipes,screen,transients}.ts`, `client/src/ui/graphics/GraphicsPanel.tsx`; new `combat/flashSafety{,.test}.ts`, `stores/accessibility.ts`, `tools/playtest/flash-audit.mjs`.

## Playtest 2: shared basic attacks ✅ (branch `pt2/kill-flash`)

Nicole: "make basic weapon attacks like wand attack viewable to everyone like the spell coming out of it". Client only: no contract, protocol, server or migration change, **nothing new on the wire** (Event 214 already names the move, and so the weapon, for every basic attack; CR 022), so the scale wire digest and the 150 kbps budget are untouched by construction.

**What already replicated** (CR 022): the attacker's avatar swing / draw / bolt poses, Event 214 for every weapon move to players within 64 m, others' swing arcs, arrows and staff bolts from the weapon tip, slams.

**The gaps** (two-browser check, `tools/playtest/shared-attacks-shots.mjs`, B ~7 m to A's side, both pages recorded with the DevTools screencast and an in-page probe of each FX engine):
- **Every weapon shot was the same 10 cm steel needle**, yours and everyone else's: a wand bolt read as a white glint, nothing like a spell leaving the wand.
- **Others saw your attack about half a second late**: B drew A's wand bolt 578 ms after A let go, the staff bolt 615 ms, the arrow 537 ms, the sword arc 565 ms (A: 25-103 ms), and the wand bolt was on B's screen for 52 ms. A player standing still sends no position updates (delta snapshots), so the interpolation stretches their delay toward its cap, and CR 022 plays their events at that delay.
- Someone else's sword arc used the calm first-person density (calm kills), thin from metres away.

**Fixes**:
- `combat/fx/weaponShot.ts`: one mapping for both sides (`weaponShotVisual(weapon, gem)`): a **wand or staff bolt is a small magic bolt** (an orb core with a halo, trail, muzzle and impact) in the socketed gem's element, a soft arcane blue without one, the staff's bigger; **an arrow a needle with a steel (or gem) trail**. Your shot (`runtime.launch`) and another player's (`engine.remoteMove` from Event 214, the move id gives the weapon, their gem the colour) use the same call. Basic attacks stay quiet next to spells (no pooled light per shot) and go through the calm-kills flash rules.
- `combat/castState.ts eventDelayMs`: **a weapon move from a caster drawn standing still plays after at most 150 ms** (`INTERP_DELAY_NEAR_MS` + 50) instead of their stretched delay; a moving caster keeps CR 022's drawn-moment timing so arm and shot stay in step, and spells, holds and dashes are unchanged.
- Someone else's swing arc (and yours in third person) keeps 70 % of its streak (steel-coloured, never white-hot); your own first-person arc stays calm.

**Measured** (`docs/evidence/pt2-shared-attacks-{before,after}.json`, the sheets `pt2-shared-attacks-{before,after}.jpg`: A's view | B's view per weapon): B now draws A's attack **254-301 ms after A's release (was 537-615 ms)**, Events 214 reached B for every attack in both runs, and B's frames show the bolt leaving A's wand / staff tip with its trail, the arrow in flight and the sword's arc (before: a white glint by the slime). Remaining delay: the event's trip (one snapshot interval), the move's startup steps and the 150 ms.

**Tests**: vitest `combat/fx/weaponShot.test.ts` (every shooting move maps to its weapon from the id Event 214 carries; wand / staff -> magic orb in the gem's family, arcane without; bow -> needle; one cached visual per weapon and element; magic bolts drawn bigger, arrows thin; no light), `combat/castState.test.ts` (a still caster's weapon move plays after 150 ms, never later than their delay, a moving caster's and spells keep the drawn delay; through `remoteEvent` and `flush`).

Gaps: the screenshots frame A small at 7 m with HUD cards on top (achievement toasts, tips); a wand bolt still flies only ~0.15 s at 9 m; melee hits on enemies are shown to others through Event 200 (steel sparks, the calm tint) as before.

Shared files touched (small): `client/src/combat/{runtime,castState,castState.test}.ts`, `combat/fx/engine.ts`, `client/src/net/RemotePlayers.tsx` (passes the drawn speed); new `combat/fx/weaponShot{,.test}.ts`, `tools/playtest/shared-attacks-shots.mjs`.

## Playtest 2: vertical world ✅ (branch `pt2/vertical-world`, foundation; palaces and sky activities are follow-ups)

Nicole: "ok new pivot for the game: make it vertical. there are tunnels you can take to underground vast, lit-by-crystal cave networks that are huge and breathtaking with glowing blue waters like a cave in south america or something, and also hellish areas in the depths too, and also you can fly up and go to sky islands, and sky towns, and specifically put in the legend of zelda skyward sword bamboo island minigame in here. lots of stuff like that in the sky. sky palaces too, which rightfully are their own domain when youre inside. they are large and have elaborate twists and turns and ambience." CR `docs/cr/038-vertical-world.md`; generator `worldgen/README.md` "Vertical world"; zone `server/zone/README.md` "Vertical world (playtest 2, CR 038)".

- **Generator, genVersion 3** (`worldgen/src/vertical/`): three layers as heightfield pairs on the terrain's 1 m lattice (sky: island top over bottom; Underground / Depths: floor under ceiling) plus a water / lava lattice, collided with the terrain's own triangle rule in every host. **Sky**: 3-5 archipelagos at 330-800 m with waterfalls into a cloud bank, 2-3 **sky towns** (laid out by the towns code on their island: inn, forge, stall, board, townsfolk, waypoint, an activity spot), 1-2 **sky palace** sites (huge, seen from below), sky ruins; **sky lift** crystals by the surface town nearest each archipelago, wind columns, geysers and currents. **Underground**: 4-8 marked **tunnel entrances** (standing-stone portals with glowing runes, on the map) winding 300-1100 m down to **vast caverns** (100-420 m across, 70-150 m roofs) entered on a balcony high on the wall: crystal halls (crystals that cast real light), cenotes (glowing turquoise lakes under daylight shafts you can jump down), river halls, fungal halls; columns, stalactites, veins, safe camps, passages and rivers between them. **Depths**: gates under the deepest caverns down to basalt halls around lava lakes and rivers, lava falls, vents, obsidian, demon shrines. Every genVersion 2 site keeps its index and id (vertical sites appended, layer byte 37); terrain heights unchanged (0 chunks); genVersion 2 archived (`worldgen/archive/worldgen-1dcd2ffc09618d97.wasm`, `golden-v2.json`). New exports `vertical_build`, `layer_tile`, `layer_height`.
- **Collision twins**: TS `VerticalGroundCore` (worldgen/js) and Go `server/pkg/vertical.Ground`, golden vectors in `golden.json`; movement goldens on the vertical ground (island fall to terminal speed, sky lift, tunnel to a cavern floor, cavern swim, cenote dive) replayed by Go. Constants v8: `UPDRAFT_MAX_LIFT` 10 -> 20, `TERMINAL_FALL_SPEED` 54, `UPDRAFT_FREE_LIFT` 8 (free gliding in strong updrafts and in the sky over an archipelago); the client's prediction has the same wind (`sim/stepEnv.setVerticalUpdrafts`).
- **Zone**: layer tiles cached in Go (wasm on a miss), per-entity layer, 3D interest between layers (80 m, party exempt), server fall damage (Event 1700) and lava (1701), area difficulty per layer (floors sky 10, Underground 6, Depths 20; no migration), packs per layer (cavern creatures; the Starless host's demons in the Depths with elite pay and 2.5x luck; harpies and wisps in the sky), layer veins, sky-town townsfolk in 3D, steeds refused underground / in the sky, discovery and fast travel for the new sites, vibe explorer layer filters, safe saved positions.
- **Client** (`client/src/world/vertical/`): layer tiles meshed in the chunk workers with baked glow (crystal light, lake and lava glow, cenote daylight), quadtree LOD per layer, island / cavern / Depths materials, glowing water and lava, decorations (crystals and spires with real lights from a shared light pool, columns, falls, light shafts, wind columns, clouds, portals, lifts, palaces, camps), cavern and Depths fog and heat shimmer, no sun underground, ambience (sky wind, cavern drips / reverb, crystal hum, lapping, Depths rumble, lava) and music per layer (composer `when.layer`), map layer toggle (Sky / Surface / Underground / Depths) with glyphs, vertical HUD (layer, place, fall warning, lift, heat), Events 1700-1703 notices, E prompt for the hooks.
- **Renewal**: `mintdb.RenewOfficial` 2 -> 3 keeps every site / id; characters saved within an opening's radius + 8 m go to the spawn marker (`TestRenewOfficialToVertical`); on login `safeSaved` lifts a character out of a new hole / island rock. No save-blob migration (blobs key progress by stable ids).
- **Hooks**: sky palace = structure kind 9 site (layer 1) + the descriptor's PALACE decoration; Interact 150 -> Event 1702 (sealed). Sky activity = towns prop kind 31 in every sky-town square; Interact 151 -> Event 1703. Replace the stubs in `server/zone/internal/game/vertical.go verticalInteract`.

**Evidence** (`client/src/world/vertical/dev/vertical-shots.mjs`, solo client, one headless Chromium, High, 1280x720): `docs/evidence/pt2-vertical-{tunnel-entrance,cavern-first-view,crystal-cavern,cenote,depths,sky-from-ground,sky-from-island,sky-town}.jpg` + `pt2-vertical-shots.json` (positions, tiles, triangles, frame times: p50 16.7 ms vsync everywhere; 157k-549k layer triangles). Low tier: 30-50 % fewer layer triangles, same views, p50 16.7 ms. **Scale**: `LODESTONE_WIRE_DIGEST` identical to main before and after the merge (`docs/evidence/pt2-vertical-bench.txt`; the scale worlds have no atlas, so no vertical world); BenchmarkScale cpu-Mcycles within noise on a shared machine.

**Gates** (main `41ec650` merged): `gen:check` ok; `test:golden` PASS in every host (wp01 evidence restored); `cargo test` (worldgen) ok, clippy clean; `go vet` clean in every module; `go test` green in every module (`TestZoneProcessCavesMining` failed once under load and passed alone); door vectors regenerated (sky-town doors appended, every existing vector unchanged); typecheck ok; `eslint .` clean; client vitest 105 files / 1267 tests (after merging main `08d53b6`, shared basic attacks: client only, nothing on the wire); worldgen/js vitest 28; `node scripts/whatsnew.mjs check` (35 entries, `pt2-vertical-world` first).

Gaps: crystals read pastel rather than deep blue / violet at a distance; the tunnel portal is modest from afar; the palace exterior is a placeholder model and its doors are sealed; sky activities are a stub stand; enemy models for the new creatures reuse existing families; no ambient occlusion in caverns; live world: renewing the official world to genVersion 3 regenerates towns (sky towns appended) and needs the usual deploy (not done here).

## Playtest 3: world edits (branch `pt3/world-edits`, CR 040)

Nicole's playtest: "Make it like the world is breakable like Minecraft, and building your own base is a lot more desirable." This branch breaks and reshapes the generated world and produces the materials building uses; placing pieces is `pt3/base-building` (CR 041).

**Works**

- **Chop, break, clear** (Interact 42, Events 1800-1803): axe for trees, pickaxe for rocks, anything for bushes; hit points scale with size (3-6 swings); the object is gone for everyone (a `world_edits` row, kind `object_destroyed`, plus a `WorldEdits` record), its drops land as the breaker's personal pickups (logs, sticks, resin, firewood from dead trees, stone, flint, plant fibre, wild berries), regrowth after 3-6 h (trees), 8-16 h (rocks), 1-2 h (bushes), never inside a base (`WorldEditSetup.InBase` hook for CR 041). A tree tips over on the mesh and material it was drawn with and sinks away; rocks shatter; chips, leaves and dust.
- **Dig and fill** (Interact 43, Events 1805-1807, message 73 `TerrainEdits`, protocol v7): a 1.6 m brush in 0.25 m steps, slope cascade keeps pits and mounds walkable (3 steps per metre, no triangle over 47 degrees), +-6 m from the generated ground, never below water + 0.3 m, never in towns, roads, sites, structures, cave roofs or mouths; a dig pays dirt (stone from deep ground), a fill costs it (batched per player so digging is not an inventory write per swing). Edited tiles rebuild in place (only the touched, loaded ones; the closed-interval overlap covers seams at any LOD) and the dug ground turns to bare soil.
- **One surface everywhere**: `worldedit.Overlay` is installed on `worldhost.Terrain`, so movement, enemies, spawns and every wrapper see the edited ground; `WorldGround.edits` in TS. **Golden** `contracts/golden/world_edits.json` (580 samples over 700 edits across two chunk borders, cached and cold wasm path) replays bit for bit in TS; `world_edits_plan.json` replays the planner swing for swing. Generated heights, the collision goldens and the mesh cache are untouched.
- **Persistence**: removed objects in `world_edits`, terrain blocks in `terrain_edits` (migration 0031, one row per chunk, 3 bytes per edited vertex), restored before the zone ticks; bounded at 640 edited chunks and 30,000 cleared objects per zone.
- **UX**: key = cycles chop and break / dig / fill / away; hold the left mouse button; a ring shows the strike, a hit bar the object's health, the HUD the depth and your dirt. New items `wood:log/stick/resin`, `stone:block/flint`, `earth:dirt`, `plant:fiber/berries`, tools `tool:axe` and `tool:shovel` (starter kit; older characters get them once), camp recipes for replacements.
- **Per-tick cost is flat** (event-driven: one comparison against the earliest regrow time, a tiny pending-grant map, the half-second interest scan).

**Evidence**: `client/src/world/edits/dev/edits-shots.ts` (real client + zone binary): `docs/evidence/pt3-world-edits-{before,clearing,digging,pit,pit-filled}.png`, `pt3-world-edits-shots.txt`.

**Tests**: `go test ./pkg/worldedit ./pkg/worldhost` (server/pkg); `go test ./internal/game -run 'Harvest|Earthwork|Tools|Starter'` (server/zone: felling with drops, persistence and restart, regrowth, reach, tools, dirt economy, TerrainEdits mirrored to a client overlay, protected ground); vitest `src/world/edits` (overlay golden, planner golden, tile build equals the edited collision surface, codec).

**Gaps**

- Cluster zones: an edit made on one zone of a WP16 cluster reaches the others at their next restart (no bus feed yet); each zone only streams its own players' edits live.
- The sounds reuse the mining and step recordings pitched down (new cues `work_*`); real chop, crash and dig recordings are a follow-up. The swing animation is the pickaxe's for every tool.
- A felled tree leaves no stump or log; grass tufts and the dense grass blades around a chopped tree stay; no collision for trees or rocks exists in the game yet, so none was changed.
- Protected ground is a coarse 4 m mask: an edit stops a few metres short of a road or a wall, and the sky islands' undersides are not checked (fills are capped at 6 m, far below any island).
- Far (LOD 2+) tiles are rebuilt too, but terrain edits are only streamed within 6 chunks of a player, so a distant pit shows when you approach.
- Digging and filling near a player's base is allowed on purpose (levelling the ground); CR 041 may add ownership.
## Playtest 3: kill flash on Linux Chrome (branch `pt3/kill-flash`)

Report: "a flashing every time myself or my friend gets a kill, on her screen (Chrome on Linux)". Client only.

**Deployed build**: the live client (`lodestone-rpg-game.netlify.app`) already contains "Calm kills" (its bundle has `lodestone_accessibility`, the flash profile and `deathEdge`), so this is not the old build.

**Found**: the spell screen effect (`combat/fx/screen.ts`, merged into the world's EffectPass at medium and up) warped the whole frame's UVs for every shockwave with `exp(-pow((r - w.z) / 0.035, 2.0))`. The base is negative inside the ring, and GLSL leaves `pow(x < 0, y)` undefined: Mesa and NVIDIA GL compile it to `exp2(y * log2(x))` = NaN, ANGLE/D3D (the Windows dev machines) gives `x * x`. NaN UVs sample garbage / black, so each kill-time shockwave (spell kills, heavy hits, explosions, deaths such as Shatter; yours and your friend's, since remote casts run the same recipes) would show a growing black / garbled disc on Linux only. Same pattern in the world-space shockwave ring (`fx/materials.ts`). **Fixed**: squared by multiplication; the other `pow()` bases in the FX materials clamped (`max(x, 0.0)`, a `1 - abs(dot)` can round below 0). `combat/glslSafety.test.ts` bans a possibly negative `pow()` base in the combat, post and loot shaders.

**Checked and ruled out** (headless Chromium, `combat/dev/program-audit.mjs`; the enemy and FX galleries now warm up through the world's `warmShaders` and list linked programs): no shader program is linked after the warm-up at an enemy death (slimes, beasts, humanoids, golem) or at any of the 92 spells' casts and impacts; the pooled point-light counts are fixed (intensity to 0, never added or removed); no whole-frame DOM overlay or CSS animation is triggered by a kill.

**Confidence**: the NaN is a real bug that fits "Linux only, every kill, both players' kills" and is fixed, but it could not be reproduced here (SwiftShader on Windows evaluates the same shader without NaN), so it is unconfirmed on her machine, roughly 60 %. If it persists: ask for the browser's `chrome://gpu` and whether Reduce flashing is still on (Graphics screen), and try Low graphics (no screen pass).

Works: fix + guard test; program audit harness. Gaps: no Linux GPU available to verify; the first instanced shadow caster still links its depth program on first sight (not at kills; the warm-up does not cover shadow depth variants).

## Playtest 3: title cavern and character creation (branch `pt3/title-creation`, CR 047)

Nicole: "Make a landing page that looks like gems and all that in a cave, then have the character selection/creation screen be like Valheim instead of plain like ours." CR `docs/cr/047-title-creation.md`.

Works:
- **Title**: logotype over a live R3F gem cavern (procedural shader rock with ore veins and wet patches, clusters in the five gem colours, light pools, shafts, fog, motes, slow camera drift), lazy chunk over a CSS cavern painted at first paint; CSS-only fallback without WebGL / on a software renderer / `?cave=0`; lite tier on phones and weak GPUs; calm under Reduce flashing and prefers-reduced-motion.
- **Character select / creation**: the same cavern with a campfire, your character (the game's own Avatar, idle animation) framed on a stage, drag to turn, list on the left with Play / Delete (confirmed) / New, creator on the right (build, skin, hair style and colour, beard, eyes, Randomise) with a live preview; phone layout (figure over one scrolling sheet). The sign-in panel and the waking screen sit over the cavern too.
- **Look**: six options rendered by `Avatar.setLook` (skin tone, 6 hair styles, hair colour, 5 beards, eye colour, 3 builds); persisted with the character through the existing `appearance` field (account: `characters.appearance`, local: the save state), returned by the character list, drawn on your own body in third person. New `DELETE /v1/characters/{id}`. No migration (0035 unused).
- Every harness testid kept; `client/scripts/title-flow.mjs` drives title -> local profile -> create (with a look) -> quick-play row -> delete against a mocked account service and checks them (10 PASS).

Gaps:
- Replication (follow-up): other players see your look through Interact 91 / Event 1900 (the snapshot mask is full, so no protocol field; no version bump). First-person hands take your skin tone, but only recolour on a page reload after switching characters. Ghosts from another zone keep the default figure.
- `TestAccountFlow` (appearance + delete) and the zone look tests ran against real Postgres/Redis. The services-backed acceptance harnesses and `infra/smoke-live.mjs` were not run; their selectors were kept rather than changed.
- The campfire sits mostly behind the left panel at 16:9; the long hair style reads as a hood; no shadows on the stage (a blob under the figure).

## Playtest 3: balance (branch `pt3/balance`, CR 048)

Nicole's playtest-3 notes, four independent fixes, one commit each (`docs/cr/048-playtest-3-balance.md`).

**Works**
- **Levelling**: level 2 costs 360 XP (was 100): the early curve is 360 / 300 / 260 / 240, plain 60 + 40 x level from level 5, so no later level is slower. Server `explore.XPForNextLevel`, client twin `xpForLevel`; wire vectors and discovery / tower tests updated (a tower climb + its discovery, 190 XP, no longer levels).
- **Magma dash tiers**: Lesser (L1-7: short, a steered run that falls, 40 mana/s hold), Honed (L8-14: weak thrust, 32 mana/s), Full (L15+, the old dash, 24 mana/s). Tiers are plain dash variants (ids 10, 11) chosen from the character level on both sides, so the shared movement twins are untouched; wp13 dash goldens regenerated, Go twin tests green; the zone refuses a tier above the caster's level; the spell card shows "Tier: Lesser (Honed at level 8)".
- **Spell bank**: equipped spells of the edited loadout are hidden; clearing a slot (or dropping its card on the bank) returns them (`bankCards`, vitest).
- **Demons**: `demons.ordinary {halfFrom 8, immuneFrom 15, halfMult 0.5}`: level < 8 takes full lasting damage from anything, 8-14 half (resisted flag), 15+ / lords / Guarded story demons keep the sunsteel-or-Light rule; HUD hint, tips and loadout lines no longer say all demons are immune.

**Tests**: `go test` explore, combat, combatdefs, movement, plus the game packages' night / dash / achievement / progression / explore tests; client vitest for sim, combat, ui/skills, ui/spellcards, ui/progression; `tsc`, eslint.

**Gaps**: `tools/acceptance/g4.mjs` and `server/zone/cmd/nightbots` (not re-run, 11 min) still assume a level-1 demon shrugs off the starting sword, true now only at demon level 15+; a character saved at level 2-4 re-levels one level lower from its kept XP; the dash tiers and demon thresholds are first guesses to tune in play.


## Playtest 3: torches and familiar lights ✅ (branch `pt3/lights`, CR 042)

Playtest: "Add stuff to light up dark areas like a torch, and a different spell for each class of magic, like a floating glowing icicle that follows you." Details: `docs/cr/042-lights.md`.

Works:
- **Torch**: the existing `tool:torch` (6 gold at general merchants, 2 firewood at a workbench) is in the starter kit and is lit with key **8** (Interact 160). First person: its own fist and forearm in the off hand; third person and every other player: in the avatar's left hand. A warm flickering pool of light (three slow sines, 8% swing, 3% with Reduce flashing; the largest per-frame step is under 1%, tested). Burns without fuel; the water (swimming), a fall, or losing the torch puts it out (zone-checked once a second; refused while swimming).
- **Familiar lights**: one cheap toggle spell at the root of each of the eight trees (type `defensive` with a `familiar` block, so no new spell type; 4 mana to call, free to dismiss, 2.5-3 mana/s while it burns, it fades at 0 mana): Hearth Mote (ember mote), Rime Light (spinning icicle), Dusk Lantern (violet lantern), Firefly Drift (fireflies), Static Orb (soft crackling ball), Zephyr Mote (ribbons of air), Lumen Wisp (radiant wisp), Starless Lamp (void lamp). Floats over the right shoulder, trailing a little.
- **Replication**: the light rides in the player's snapshot record (`EntityRecord.appearance` bits 51-56, next to CR 034's armor): other players see your torch and familiar and are lit by them; late joiners, range entry and ghosts get it on first sight; nothing is sent per tick. Events 1840/1841, Interact 160.
- **Lighting tech**: a fixed pool of PointLights (2 low / 3 medium / 4 high+ultra), created before the shader warm-up and never added or removed (the scene held 34 lights before and after the lights were lit: `__lodestoneLights()`), ranked each frame (yours first, then nearest, nothing past 48 m), plus an emissive glow (additive halo) on every source. Terrain, caves, layers and towns are all `MeshStandardMaterial`s, so they take the pooled lights unchanged. Warm-up objects for the new programs are in `warmExtras.ts`.
- Zone: `server/zone/internal/game/lights.go`, `lights_test.go` (torch lights, replicates to an observer and a late joiner, goes out in the water, cannot be lit while swimming; familiar called, replicated, upkeep state, dismissed free at 0 mana, fades when the mana runs dry). Client: `client/src/world/lights/*.test.ts` (flicker bounds, pool ranking and sizes, the eight familiars one per school and learnable at a tree root, Event 1840/1841 into the store).
- Dev: `/tp x z y` teleports to an exact height (the Underground), used by the evidence harness `client/src/world/lights/dev/lights-shots.ts`.

Evidence (`docs/evidence/pt3-lights-*`): `cave-dark`, `cave-torch`, `cave-torch-two-familiars` (a torch and two familiars in an Underground tunnel), `cave-frost-own-view`, `familiars` (all eight in third person), `pt3-lights-run.json` (pool intensities, light count, frame time).

Gaps: no ground-placed torch and no Lumen Wisp demon effect (the brief's optional items); torch and familiar state does not survive a zone hand-off (press 8 / recast); older characters have no starter torch; the 80 m+ low-LOD figure shows only a halo; the eight spell names are proposals (`pending`).

## Playtest 3: towns art (branch `pt3/towns-art`, CR 046)

Playtest feedback: "Make the assets in the world better and higher fidelity, like it would make you want to live in this world, super immersive. Houses are just okay." Render-side only: no genVersion change, no collision or footprint change, no contract, migration or event change. Details in `docs/cr/046-towns-art.md` and `client/src/world/buildings/README.md` "Towns art".

**Works**
- **Detail levels** (`buildings/detail.ts`) follow the quality tier at startup: low = the old look and cost, medium adds frames / trim / dressing, high adds the rich pass and 512 px textures.
- **Houses**: windows with frames, leaded panes (warm per-pane glow at night, emissive, no lights), board-and-batten shutters (some open), flower boxes, curtains; stone footing courses; lime wash per building; real half-timbering on the timber style (posts, plates, rails, knee braces, framed gables); per-building roof tint, ridge caps, rafter tails; stepped chimneys with pots (smoke from the pots); door surrounds, glowing wall lanterns, the inn's hanging sign.
- **Settlement**: yard clutter (barrels, crates, sacks, wood piles, hay, carts, wash lines, ladders, rain barrels, benches, coal, doorstep pots) placed against side and back walls, clear of every collision box, road and the square (`dress.ts` `Yard`); stall valances and hanging produce, well bucket and windlass, pointed fence caps, town wall arrow slits and buttresses, gate pennants, signpost cairns.
- **Interiors**: skirting, plastered ceilings between dark beams, wainscot, curtains, hanging herbs, an inn's candle wheel and table candles (flames are emissive and flicker), bed posts and footboards, table runners, hearth mantel with cauldron and logs.
- **Textures**: painters are resolution independent (256 px low / medium, 512 px high): lime-wash plaster with damp and hairline cracks, chiselled stone with pits, board knots and nails, glazed tile lichen, silvered shingles, leaded window picture, lighter fieldstone.

**Measured** (`docs/evidence/pt3-towns-after-drawcalls.{txt,json}`; headless Chromium, RTX 3070 via ANGLE/D3D11, 1600x900, vite dev server, Draedmore, 40 buildings, clear sky). Settlement shell / interior triangles (`art.test.ts`): low 21.2k / 12.8k, medium 42.2k / 22.5k, high 63.9k / 29.6k; build time ~0.1 s per large town at every level (worker). Draw calls are unchanged by the pass (the new geometry is merged into the same shell / interior meshes): high street view 358 calls (256 without the towns layer; before 354), overview 332, interior 353; medium street 263, low street 149 (no shadow passes; the old low cost). Whole-frame triangles on the street view: before 2050k, after 2439k at high (shadow passes included); medium 1522k, low 654k. Mean frame 8-11 ms uncapped on the 3070 at every tier (headless, noisy; before ~9-10 ms). 512 px textures: ~4.7 s to paint in the worker at startup on high (1.2-1.5 s at 256 px), 34 MB vs 8.6 MB of texture memory before mips; they are high-tier only. Screenshots (`pt3-towns-{before,after}-{street,street-dusk,interior,interior-house}.jpg`, plus `pt3-towns-after-{styles-timber,town-night}.jpg`): the same street by day and at dusk, an inn and a house interior.

**Gates**: `npx tsc -p client --noEmit` clean; `eslint client/src/world/buildings` clean; vitest `src/world/buildings` 9 files all pass, including `kit.test.ts` (every wall / floor / roof face still drawn on its collision box), `doors.test.ts` (door vectors bit for bit) and both zone-process integration tests (walk into a building, walk through a door: 0 corrections); new `art.test.ts` (detail-level budgets, valid meshes, yard clearance query, determinism, seed 3 adobe).

**Gaps**
- Yard clutter and the new trim have no collision (like porch posts); the zone does not know about them, so a player can walk through a barrel against a wall.
- Detail follows the tier the session started with; settlements already streamed in keep their level until they are rebuilt (auto tier changes do not rebuild).
- Not touched: exploration / structure towers (collision overlay twins), bridges, docks, roads and plaza paving, NPC models, stall buildings beyond their props. Weather and wind do not move the new cloth (wash lines, pennants).
- Interiors are still lit by the sky and the fixed light pool: candle flames and glass are emissive, nothing casts light.
- 512 px textures add ~4 s of worker time at startup on high before towns appear.

## Playtest 3: vegetation art (branch `pt3/vegetation`, CR 045)

Works: rich trees (9 species x 2 variants x 2 LODs, bark, root flare, branching, ~250 alpha-tested leaf cards, translucency, branch and leaf wind, alpha shadows, impostors baked from them), bent-blade meadow grass with flowers, ferns, reeds, shrubs, scrub, heather, fractured stratified rocks with moss and snow, by tier (Low unchanged; Medium/High/Ultra 0.6/1/1.3 x cards), dense grass masked out of towns' roads, plazas and plots. Instancing and ids unchanged (`hide(id)` works). Evidence `docs/evidence/pt3-veg-{before,after}-*`; harness `client/src/world/bench/vegetation.mjs`.

Gaps: no terrain surface change; forest draw calls 413 (> 400) and ~+1 M triangles; frame-time A/B was taken on a shared GPU (p50 12.0 vs 12.1 ms, noisy); no palms or sky/cave species (no scatter records for them).

## Playtest 3: Base building ✅ (branch `pt3/base-building`, CR 041)

Playtest: "building your own base is a lot more desirable". Extends the camps (CR 025) instead of replacing them: a building piece is a camp piece (same table, events, chests, collision set, area streaming, zone ownership), so camps keep loading unchanged. Contract: `contracts/build.yaml` (appended into the camps tables by the generator), `docs/cr/041-base-building.md`; Events 1820-1821, Interact 90 actions 11-14, refusal reasons 24-34, record version 2, migration 0032, no protocol message.

**Works**
- **Hammer (key 9) and the build menu**: 15 structural pieces (foundation, floor, wall, half wall, window wall, door, stairs, ramp, roof slope, ridge cap, gable, pillar, beam, fence, gate) in wood, stone and sunsteel-reinforced stone, 13 furnishings and base pieces (bench, table, rug, banner, lantern, wall torch, trophy mount, bed, large chest, home forge, kitchen hearth, carpenter's bench, warding hearth); grid and socket snapping, quarter turns, material switch, green/red ghost with the reason and the cost with what you are missing; hold X deconstructs (80 % back), F repairs; first-time tip.
- **Support**: pieces must connect to the ground through sockets (a foundation, or a ground piece); floors may hang two cells off a held neighbour; a deconstruct that would leave others hanging is refused.
- **Collision**: walls, doors (by state), windows and fences are boxes in the camps' solid set (TS/Go twin, golden vectors `server/pkg/camps/testdata/build.json`); foundations, floors, stairs and ramps are walkable surfaces (`camps.Level.HeightAt`), so you can live on a second floor.
- **A base worth coming home to**: the bed is the respawn point and rests with a comfort score from the furnishings within 12 m (+2.5 % Rested duration and +2 % health regen a point, tiers Bare/Cosy/Homely/Luxurious); Return home (5 minute cooldown, the fast-travel and combat rules); large chest (32 slots), forge, kitchen hearth, carpenter's bench work at home; the warding hearth keeps demons away within 20 m at night (the campfire's night rules), warms, and mends pieces; pieces weather lazily by material (nothing written as they wear) and are repaired for materials.
- **Ownership and limits**: owner-only by default, Share with party (use, repair, deconstruct, build beside); no building in or within 24 m of settlements, on roads, in sites; 450 pieces per character and 1600 per 256 m area; solid-set rebuild coalesced to once per tick; decay after 30 days away. Streamed with the camp events; rendered as one instanced mesh per piece kind and state (procedural planks, mortar-lined stone, shingles, steps), glow by emissive and the existing flame cards plus a fixed pool of two lights.
- **Evidence**: `docs/evidence/pt3-base-building-{day,day-corner,night,night-inside,ghost}.png` (a 54-piece house placed through the real rules, from the standalone lab `client/src/ui/build/dev`).

**Gaps**
- Pieces cannot be damaged (no raids) and weathering never destroys one; roofs have no collision; a chest's lock and the base's share setting are one switch.
- The ghost cannot see roads and sites (only towns): green where the zone refuses 25/26.
- The client was shot in the standalone lab, not against a live zone; the zone path is covered by `server/zone/internal/game/builds_test.go` (a whole house over the wire) and the golden vectors.
- Logs, stone and resin come from CR 040 (world-edits); this branch adds the items (`wood:log`, `wood:resin`, `stone:block`, `earth:dirt`) and a dev grant (Interact 90/9). If CR 040 ships other keys, change them in `contracts/build.yaml` (the generator checks them against items.yaml).
- Hotkey: the CR's "B" was taken (character build), so the hammer is 8 (voice's default key moved to 9).
- `BuildLayer` adds two permanent point lights on top of the camp layer's three (constant count); stone/earth items have no custom icons.

## Playtest 3: sky palaces, sky activity, creatures (branch `pt3/sky-depths`)

Playtest feedback: "Continue with the sky and underground content." CR `docs/cr/044-sky-depths.md`; Events 1704-1706 and 1890-1891, Interact 152 and 153, migration 0034; What's new `pt3-sky-depths`. No worldgen change (genVersion stays 3).

**Works**

Its walls and floors are collision boxes appended to the towns on both sides (bit-identical twin, golden test). Interior: portico, vestibule, annexes, colonnade, sun and moon galleries, an atrium with a wind shaft up to a gallery, a throne hall. Trial: strike three wards, the Sky Guardian (boss, three phases) wakes, its fall opens a chest (gold plus two relic-or-better, per character per 20 h). Co-op, server-authoritative, per-tick cost flat.
- **Glide Ring Race** at every sky town stand: twelve-ring spiral, server-checked each tick only while racing, medals pay gold, XP and a gold-medal relic, best times per stand persisted (migration 0034) with a top five.

- Deeper crystal tints, grander tunnel portal, baked cavern ambient occlusion, sky birds and wind chimes.

**Gaps**: sky-town NPC variety and a bounty-board quest, a second activity (cloud harvest) and island props were not done; the palace's doors are visual (open as you approach; the doorway is never blocked); the palace renders its interior from one merged mesh (no occlusion culling inside); the ring race has no zone-wide announcement of records; the live world needs the usual genVersion 3 renewal and migration 0034.

Evidence (`docs/evidence/`): `pt3-palace-{from-below,exterior,gate,inside,atrium,ring,throne}.jpg`, `pt3-race.jpg`, `pt3-depths-creature.jpg`.

Renewal 3 -> 4 keeps every site and id (`TestRenewOfficialToDwarves`).

## Playtest 4: lively townsfolk (CR 050, `pt4/lively-npcs`)

Nicole: "make NPCs more lively." CR: `docs/cr/050-lively-townsfolk.md`. Events 1930 (bark, wired to bubbles) and 1931 (town bell); no migration.

**Works**
- **Speech bubbles** over every live `npcFocus` line (zone barks, greetings, cries, chats, dialog lines): DOM cards, at most 4, within 25 m, nearest first, lines to you emphasised, eased in and out.
- **They notice you**: head and torso follow the player within 6 m (limited, easing back, an occasional nod); a wave and a short line within 5 m, rate limited (a few a minute); the dialog's NPC faces you, plants its feet, gestures and moves its mouth while speaking; every barking NPC flaps its mouth.
- **Work**: hammering smith with sparks and a clang on each blow (spatialised, quiet; replaces the forge's random strikes while he is in view), hoeing/scything farmers, merchants arranging wares or holding a crate and calling out, guards with spears scanning, innkeepers wiping and carrying a mug, villagers sweeping or carrying baskets/buckets, idle villagers on doorsteps. Breathing, weight shift, blinking, look-arounds, per-NPC height/build/gait, stooping elders.
- **Looks**: 5 hair styles, bald, hats, caps, hoods, headscarves, beards, vests, aprons, tabards, skirts, sleeves long or rolled, in single-draw gear meshes.
- **Each other**: idle neighbours within 3.4 m chat in alternating bubbles from 22 overheard conversations (regional gossip), deterministic from ids and the clock.
- **Reactions**: rain (hand over head, villagers and farmers go indoors), dusk (a guard calls "Lanterns!" via `npcBark`, once per town per night; the town bell, Event 1931, synthesized cue), monsters nearby (villagers cower, guards face them), level-up (cheer and a line).
- **Schedules** (`npc/life.go`, pure, tested on the official world): lunch at the inn, an evening gathering at the square and inn, merchant market hours, guard beats, strolls to neighbours, doorstep rests, rain shelter. Every goal is reached through streets and doors (walk tests).
- **Town animals**: chickens, dogs (one tags along), cats; four draws, halved on Low.

**Numbers** (largest-NPC square of Draedmore on the official seed, headless Chromium on the shared GPU, `client/src/world/buildings/dev/lively-shots.mjs --only bench`, `docs/evidence/pt4-lively-{before,after}-bench.json`; frame times swing 2x with the other agents' load, so read draw calls):
- NPC meshes: 11 before, 8 in view now plus sparks only while a smith strikes. Draw calls in the same view with 22 NPCs: before 330 (325 with the townsfolk hidden), after 339 (325 hidden; 8 in the main pass plus 6 in the shadow passes). Run-to-run noise on the total is about +-5 calls.
- Zone: `BenchmarkStepActiveTown` (every NPC of the largest settlement, one tick): about 15-20 ns per NPC per tick before, 19-28 after (400 active NPCs: about 3 us of a 33 ms tick). `go test ./internal/npc ./internal/game -run "Town|Dusk|Rain|NPC|Door"` pass.
- Evidence: `docs/evidence/pt4-lively-{smith,chat,look,animals}.jpg`.

**Gaps**
- Greetings, chats, cries and reactions are client-side only: two players may see different chats in the same minute only if their NPC ids differ (towns-buffer records are used when the NPC matches one, so normally they agree), and the zone does not know NPCs are chatting (a walking NPC breaks off a chat; nothing makes them stop for it). The client cannot stop an NPC the zone is walking.
- Cowering and "head for shelter" are visual or by the schedule only; NPCs do not path to the nearest doorway when it starts raining (they go home).
- The blacksmith screenshot is taken from inside the forge (the open front puts the camera in the wall otherwise); props are blocky and their grips are tuned by eye.
- Sitting is only for idle villagers found on a doorstep; there are no benches outdoors in towns (all town benches are indoors).
- Animals are not drawn in dwarven halls or sky towns; dogs do not sit, cats have no tail swish, chickens do not flock.
- The dusk call needs an active guard or innkeeper (a player within 200 m of the settlement).
